ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u2	We continue with the debate in section 23, Ministry for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge. For the presentation of the section, I give the floor to the name of the Government, the fourth Vice-President of the Government and Minister for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u3	Good morning, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. I rise to this rostrum to defend the draft State General Budgets Act with regard to section 23, Ministry for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge, its autonomous agencies and State water societies. And I do so from the point of view of what has been agreed in the Commission procedure, with the incorporation of the amendments that have already been voted on, amendments that do not alter at all the basic orientation and strategic axes of the initial budget of the ministry and do make some territorial adjustments towards certain autonomous communities, which will be able to benefit from the result of the agreement of these amendments. The times we are living in are enormously complex, with a pandemic situation like the current one, with its consequences, which require global, joint, focused responses, and the European Union has understood this by launching the main recovery tool, the Recovery and Resilience Fund. This has also been understood by our country and its Government, which has developed a Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, a plan for economic and social recovery, transformation and modernization of our economy and productive sectors, which allows us to achieve greater resilience to future economic shocks and uncertainties. The budgets we present align themselves with this urgent need to revive the economy, with the ecological and energy transition as one of the most important bases of modernisation and social inclusion, incorporating the necessary items to activate the first measures of the recovery plan as soon as this budget is opened. They are the vehicle for channelling European funds, decisive for this recovery. They form the basis for a new social contract to close gaps in inequality, intergenerational gaps and territorial gaps, which have been increasing over the years. As the Minister of Finance pointed out, it is a real anomaly that we continue with extended budgets for 2018, designed in circumstances that have nothing to do with the current ones and that have been absolutely out of date. And in this scenario, the budget of the Ministry for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge is growing by 132%. We are talking about a budget of EUR 12 307 million, an increase due to the inclusion of appropriations from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Fund. In the case of the Ministry for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge, it amounts to 6805 million euros by 2021, 55% of the department's budget. These figures mean that of the EUR 60 billion that the European Union has planned for Spain in terms of transfers, 10% will be incorporated in the ministry next year, or that of the EUR 27 000 that will be incorporated into the general state budgets, 25% will be managed by the ministry. We are facing a great challenge, but above all a great opportunity for transformation and change in which we must all feel concerned. This is because the European Commission states that, with a view to recovery, the sensible and intelligent thing is that at least 37% of the funds are earmarked for the energy transition and the ecological transition, for climate action. The most important item of expenditure is the just and inclusive energy transition, amounting to €9774 million, of which €5390 corresponds to the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. The programme entitled Energy Transition, Recovery and Resilience Facility - with €53 billion in Chapter 7 - will target electricity infrastructure, smart grid promotion and storage deployment; measures for sustainable, safe and connected mobility in urban and metropolitan environments; actions to rehabilitate buildings and housing, and urban regeneration in areas of population decline; the overall deployment of renewable energy, including innovative renewables such as marine wind or biogas; actions to boost the energy value chain; and the development of a specific strategy to achieve 100 per cent renewable islands. In this regard, I would like to highlight the 600 million earmarked for green hydrogen as a country project or the other 600 million earmarked for the renovation of buildings and properties of the General Administration of the State, especially in areas linked to the demographic challenge. Of the €5390 million, €90 million, for the Just Transition Institute, aimed at economic dynamism and identifying opportunities and generating employability in areas affected by the closure of thermal and nuclear power plants, it shows more than evident from the Government's commitment to a just and inclusive energy transition. With regard to the Ministry's own budget, the appropriations allocated to the Institute for Diversification and Energy Saving represent an increase of 80% over the 2018 extended budget; in particular, the budget is increased to finance technical assistance, studies and service activities, reflecting the workload related to the proper implementation of the National Integrated Energy and Climate Plan and the recovery plan, promoting the action of all public and private actors. Taking into account that the European Regional Development Funds transfer expenditure once certified in Chapter 8, the Institute for Diversification and Energy Saving is provided with a credit of up to 300 million for advances to boost renewable energy and energy efficiency. We also increased the allocation of the fund for the thermal social bond, experiencing a 16% increase over the previous year. In the field of mining, a budget of EUR 14.3 million includes various concepts for rehabilitation of mining waste facilities, research in the field of health and safety of extractive work. In addition, we find both current and capital transfers to both the National Commission on Markets and Competition and the Institute for the Fair Transition. Capital transfers, EUR 3597.8 million to the NCMC, are increased by 6%. This increase is due to the incorporation of two new concepts: 202.7 million to compensate for the fall in revenues in the electricity system and in the supply of natural gas resulting from the urgent measures adopted by the Government under Royal Decree 11/2020, the additional urgent measures in the social and economic field to deal with the COVID, that is to say to respond to the prohibition of cuts in supplies that we live in during the months of confinement. As a result of these measures, more than 120 000 self-employed persons and companies have benefited from a reduction in the contracted power of electricity and gas and 30 000 from the temporary suspension of energy supply contracts. I would like to highlight in relation to this item aimed at the liquidation of the costs of the system that the electricity system is designed for a context of high prices and rising demand, a scenario very different from that promoted from the policies of energy transition to which we advance and which leads us to low and competitive prices, dominated by renewables and energy efficiency. It is therefore time for reflection, shared by different sectors and parliamentary groups. That is why, in addition to this budget allocation, I trust that we will soon be able to put forward a proposal to give adequate signals to consumers and investors, signals consistent with the decarbonisation and the necessary energy transition, giving way to stability, to the reliability of investors in the electricity system. On the other hand, one of the first commitments made by this Government in the field of energy is to articulate a just transition that leaves no one behind, as I said before. For this reason, the budget foreseen for the institute, including the 90 million euros of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, is 422.17 million euros, 25.12% more than in the 2019 extended budget. To this figure will be added the European Fair Transition Funds, as well as the funds of other bodies that, like IDAE, prioritize their calls in areas in transition, so the aid for transitional areas will increase considerably. The chapter linked to the economic development of the coal regions is endowed with a budget of 285 million to facilitate the creation of industrial, economic, business and employability opportunities in this area. For the new challenges related to the closure of thermal or nuclear power plants, I would like to highlight that for the first time we have incorporated a budget line of EUR 166 million of government own funds to be transferred to the institute or that for the environmental restoration, through the various spending programmes, will be allocated EUR 121.2 million in mining areas or areas where plants are closed, compared to the EUR 25 million provided for in the previous budget. On the other hand, the ministry's budget includes those allocated to the environment, 2452 million, of which 1415 correspond to the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. The general budgets of the State concerning the Secretariat of State for the Environment respond to the commitments that Spain has made at international level, both within the framework of the Paris Agreement and in the implementation of the European Green Pact, and they do so as a safe bet for a better society, for a more reasonable future and with more opportunities for our children and grandchildren. As the Government has pointed out, these budgets will enable us to implement green policies to meet the decarbonization objectives, to build an economy, a society, cities that are much more resilient to the effects of climate change, to ensure the availability of something as precious as water or the protection of biodiversity or our coastline. We modernise our economy, facilitate more intelligent forms of production and consumption, modernise our industrial fabric on the line marked in a transversal way by the 2030 Agenda. The Spanish Climate Change Office addresses among its most immediate challenges the implementation of part of the instruments of the Strategic Framework for Energy and Climate, in particular the recently approved II National Plan for Adaptation to Climate Change or the impetus to the Long-Term Decarbonization Strategy, which marks the path to achieving climate neutrality by 2050. The office has a budget of €24.5 million, which is distributed in lines of support to territorial administrations and the Carbon Fund for support to actions in the area of emission reduction. A plan for the modernization of the State Meteorology Agency is also being discussed, with a budget of more than 144 million euros, because we need to understand, observe and predict the evolution of our climate. More than EUR 20 million is earmarked for the creation of a new generation of weather monitoring network that will provide more accurate weather forecasts and anticipate the emergence of adverse events, thus improving the capacity to plan actions to address the challenges of climate change impacts. For its part, the Directorate-General for Biodiversity, Forests and Desertification is constituted as a new management centre integrating previously dispersed competencies in different areas of the ministry and in other ministries; with a budget of 793 million euros it represents a very important commitment for the improvement of our natural heritage, with a comprehensive approach aligned with the recently approved European Biodiversity Strategy and that will be collected at national level in our future strategic plan for biodiversity. The main lines addressed by this heading are the conservation of biodiversity, both marine and terrestrial, the improvement of knowledge of natural heritage, as well as an important exercise in the restoration of degraded ecosystems, such as wetland recovery, recovery of areas affected by mining or urban green infrastructure. In forestry, forest management and forest mass improvement are promoted and the protection of our natural environment is ensured, particularly in the face of the terrible threat of fires and through the control of international trade in species. Think, ladies and gentlemen, that Spain is one of the hot spots in the illegal trafficking of species. In short, a very ambitious package of actions, which, in addition to addressing the current biodiversity crisis with determination, has a special positive impact on the demographic challenge. For the recovery of degraded spaces, the adaptation of the coast to climate change, the protection and recovery of coastal ecosystems, accessibility, recovery and delimitation of the maritime-terrestrial public domain, nature-based solutions and the protection of the sea, the General Directorate of the Coast and the Sea will have 210 million euros, which will be distributed in actions that aim to increase the resilience of the coast to the effects of climate change by improving its protection, actions for the management of the maritime-terrestrial public domain and the implementation, full operation, of marine strategies and plans for the management of the maritime space. In the Directorate-General for Water, the budget for 2021 amounts to EUR 1780 million and includes the autonomous bodies, i.e. the hydrographic confederations and the Commonwealth of the Taibilla Canals, and the State water, Aquaes and Aquamed societies. I would like to point out that by 2021 the transfers from the Directorate-General for Water to confederations will increase by 42% over the current budget. In this area, the new planning cycle, watershed hydrological plans to be approved in the course of 2021 together with the Flood Risk Management Plan, is in advanced processing process. It is also currently in public consultation and will facilitate the management of the implementation of the budget. The Water Treatment, Sanitation, Efficiency, Saving and Reuse Plan, which our country cannot delay any longer, has been accumulating delays for too long. And I would like to make special mention of the clean-up plan, which is aimed at treating waste water in agglomerations with less than 5000 inhabitants equivalent in the context of the demographic challenge; it is those small centres that need the most help from the State. We need this initiative to maintain its vocation of continuity over time. Initially, this endowment for small municipalities has a specific reserve of 100 million euros. In order to address the implementation of the Spanish Strategy for Circular Economy, the Directorate-General for Quality and Environmental Assessment has a budget of EUR 500 million, practically implemented through transfers to territorial administrations and the industrial sectors, which must strengthen the lines defined as priorities in the strategy through its action, through the transformation of their production systems or the use of new materials. This same Directorate-General incorporates a budget of over EUR 14 million for the protection and improvement of the environment, of which EUR 7 million is earmarked for investments aimed at speeding up the state's environmental assessment procedures. The Government has also identified among its priorities the demographic challenge and the fight against depopulation. It is the first time that a governmental mechanism has been articulated to address the problems of territorial cohesion, and for that reason the General Secretariat for the Demographic Challenge has for the first time a specific item so that from the secretariat itself, through the general budgets, they can facilitate and act as a lever to encourage new investment projects that better understand where and how to test mechanisms that allow us to ensure the effectiveness of each of the items that cross-cuttingly are intended to address this accumulated inequality over decades between citizens depending on where they live. To this item, of 17 million euros for investment projects, are added the funds of the plan of recovery transformation and resilience of the Spanish economy that, through the different chapters, mobilizes more than 25 billion euros in projects focused on economic revival in areas affected by depopulation. The demographic challenge is incorporated across budgets, aligning the actions of all ministerial departments in more than 40 budget programmes to achieve the objectives set out in the National Strategy against the Demographic Challenge. So this budget is a turning point that we want to consolidate not only this year, but also in the years and years ahead. It will be a complex but fundamental and important task and the citizens are waiting for us together. I'm done. We are addressing the present and future of Spaniards and Spaniards, protagonists and recipients of public accounts that seek to give certainty at a time of extraordinary uncertainty; certainty, confidence and hope that we can emerge from this crisis by really laying the foundations for solid, fair, inclusive and sustainable growth; in which we have to move forward in the necessary modernization of our productive system while generating greater social cohesion and fighting accumulated inequalities; in short, in which we work to ensure that no one is left behind. We are going to address an enormously exciting challenge, an unprecedented challenge that will require the mobilization, the proper implementation of an extraordinary amount of resources, including administrative resources, and a regulatory adequacy to reduce potential barriers and speed up procedures to ensure that the development of this set of actions is swift and efficient, without losing any guarantee in relation to the principles of integrity, transparency and accountability or environmental protection. It is imperative that we take advantage of this unique opportunity and that means coordinated work between administrations, with the active participation of the business community and of civil society as a whole. As the President of the Government said, they are unalterable budgets, essential budgets for the modernization and recovery of our economy, and also for justice and equity; they are the budgets that our country needs and, therefore, I ask your ladies and gentlemen to support this section as well. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

However, the appropriations for the General Secretariat of the Demographic Challenge are only EUR 17 million. In our view, it is a minimum amount, although we certainly hope - we have heard the words of the Minister of Finance - that the programme will grow in the future. We expect it - it is necessary - because 17 million for challenge size seems a negligible amount. It is true, as we are told - and we share it - that the policy of combating depopulation or the demographic challenge can never be a sectoral policy. Indeed, the failure of policies to date in this struggle has been due to their sectoral nature. It is the policy that many of the autonomous communities apply and that they have used other previous governments, above with very small budgetary allocations. Indeed, politics must be cross-cutting and must permeate all ministries, so most of our amendments in this field were aimed at creating an inter-ministerial plan to combat depopulation. These amendments have almost all been vetoed by the Government because they entailed an increase in expenditure; although we had provided a way of including new revenue, it seems that it was not the proper budgetary technique. However, the objective remains that; we believe that the items should be substantially increased and imbued with the entire ministry, and that is why in one of our amendments - a non-economic amendment - we call for a cross-sectional reading of the actions of all ministries and for all this to be given the form of a plan for the reactivation of unpopulated areas, as mandated by this House in a motion adopted in June, so that, on the one hand, we can see the action of the State against depopulation more effectively and, on the other hand, we can monitor its implementation and evaluate the outcome of those policies. We know that this is an important administrative work, but it is worth making a contribution to making the entire action of the State visible, because if not a policy that has been defined as one of the four pillars on which the plan of reactivation and recovery of the country is based would be somewhat blurred in the actions of the various ministries; we believe that it deserves the opportunity of such an overall assessment of the set of actions, as you mentioned a moment ago; I believe that you have spoken of forty measures or forty budget groups. This policy, which is not sectoral, but inter-ministerial, must reflect greater ambition. If any glue we put to the actions that have been described is that we miss a greater ambition. We also recognize that it is the first year that a specific item is applied, the first year that budgets consider the problem to be a problem to be addressed, but we demand greater ambition because the problem is so serious, so - we would say - so profound and complex its solution that we cannot fail to try as hard as possible. Among the amendments that remain alive to this section, we attach particular importance to one that refers to support for public employment of the Administration in the rural world and in areas with depopulation problems. Many positions of public officials or public employees remain un covered, in an interim situation; the person who goes there is unable to settle, so we propose a specific destination supplement to support this maintenance. It is also very important - they are not matters directly from his ministry, but they are very related to him - that, in the face of the abandonment of small peoples by the banking entities, in line with an amendment that presented his group precisely in the Senate, multi-entity cashiers are configured and distributed throughout all localities that cease to have banking services. Special attention - he has also mentioned - deserves us the Just Transition Conventions, as they are perhaps the essential tool for the ecological transition process to be seen by society as an absorbable process and not having a substantial impact on the future of the territories. The future of all workers and the future of economic activity in all the regions concerned must be guaranteed and I must say that we have also missed a greater budget for these agreements here. ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u62	Thank you very much, Mr. Elorza. We continue with the debate in Section 24, Ministry of Culture and Sport. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Culture and Sport.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u63	With venom, the president. Ladies and gentlemen, almost a year ago, for the first time in our country's recent democratic history, a coalition government was formed and I am very honoured to be a part of it. This Government refused, we refuse, to face the pandemic crisis with austerity policies and cuts, and we put all the machinery of the State at the service of solidarity, of rescuing people and maintaining their jobs, without leaving anyone behind. The door to the crisis is guided by a marked social accent, by a feeling of empathy, civic virtue that we need to cultivate all, not only for reasons of justice, but also of pure rationality, in order to ensure coexistence and social peace. It can be said, ladies and gentlemen, that the draft General State Budgets presented in this House is a faithful expression of this ethical and political orientation. By focusing specifically on culture, I am not tired of repeating that it is necessary, that culture is necessary and that culture is safe. Its importance lies in its triple dimension - which I want to recall here -: firstly, as the right of all people, both of those who make it - artists, writers, creators - and of those who consume it, the citizens in general. Copyright and universal access to culture are, respectively, two of its most significant achievements. Secondly, culture is also an industry, an industry that generates wealth and employment. And, thirdly - I think it is opportune to say this after what we have heard - culture is our system of values, built throughout history, expression of the critical and democratic thinking of the struggle for rights - I would say Ihering - concrete in rules and principles, in traditions, customs feelings and beliefs that make up our vision of the world, our public ethics and that define us as a country. These values point to a Spain open to Europe and the world, civil, modern, humanist, based on the rule of law, democracy and the recognition and guarantee of the fundamental rights of all. Unfortunately, culture, in the three senses pointed out, has been severely and severely beaten during these months. The measures adopted on limitation of opportunities and mobility, although they have been essential from the perspective of public health, have made it difficult, if not impeded, for live artistic performances and the face-to-face consumption of culture. Let us recognize that these necessary limitations have led, and continue to do so, to major economic damage to the cultural industries as a whole and to cultural workers, which we have helped to alleviate with two decrees, one in May and the other in November, validated by this House. The pandemic has also distorted our system of values, altered priorities and generated mistrust, fears, not a small rift, and enthroned uncertainty as an omnipresent factor in our future perspective. Now we all feel more vulnerable and aware of our fragility. It is true that other values have emerged that we must cultivate and extend, such as fraternity, the desire for harmony and unity, civic friendship and that social empathy to which I referred before, mutual respect, and the priority of health, life or also culture. Many citizens have experienced the harm of others, the pain of their fellow citizens as their own, and this is good, it is exemplary; this is the way. This draft General Budgets of the State is intended to help in the coming times with these mires of solidarity, cooperation, stability and determination. We want to combat uncertainty and mistrust, and to bet on certainties and trust in ourselves as a great country that we are. To this end, we look to the future, considering culture and sport as basic contents of the welfare state, of our constitutional democracy and of the citizens' rights catalogue. For this reason, culture and sport have been the subject of a relevant treatment in the preparation of these budgets of 2021. For the first time in a long time - even I would say in our history - are not the culture and sport the pagans of the crisis. And far from cuts like those in other times of crisis, your budget for 2021 is growing significantly. This is one of the commitments we have made in the Government programme; we have fulfilled, despite the pandemic or, if you prefer, we have more reason to fulfil the pandemic. The Government shows its commitment to culture and sport by including in the ten lever policies of the National Plan for Recovery, Transformation and Resilience a specific line for the impetus of the cultural and sports industries. This means providing them with an additional 206 million euros, additional for 2021. This economic injection added or complementary to the budget is an essential factor in driving the process of transforming our creative and cultural industries into a new model based on digitisation, innovation, quality, the defence of the rights of artists and creators, gender equality and sustainability. Thus, and to go into detail, the budget for 2021 in consolidated terms amounts to EUR 848.40 million. Adding the European Union's funds, the department's budget stands at more than EUR 1 billion, in particular in 1054, an increase of 37.7% over the 2018 budgets. And if we are talking about the government's cultural policies as a whole, the increase is more than 25%. Ladies and gentlemen, with these numbers, with these budgets, the Ministry of Culture and Sport responds to the sectors that have suffered the most from the crisis of COVID-19. Addressed to these sectors, including performing and musical arts, cinema, fine arts, book, author's fashion and the rest of the cultural industries or visual arts, we have recorded significant budget increases. Thus, for example, the National Institute of Performing Arts and Music, which also includes theatre, circus, dance and, of course, our flamenco, increases its endowment by 34.5 % compared to the previous year, which is 53 million euros more. 2 million euros will be dedicated to the launch of the National Center for Disseminating Dance, thus fulfilling the commitment we have acquired with one of the artistic expressions less historically supported by the cultural institutions of our country. Similarly, the cinema and audiovisual sector, the Spanish hub we want to build, are reinforced through the budget of the Institute of Cinematography and Audiovisual Arts, which increases its endowment by 34 million to 121.6 million euros. We maintain the Cinematography Protection Fund, endowed with almost 70 million euros and on which depends the call of the usual lines of help of the ICAA to the production of films, as well as aids to the distribution, to the participation in festivals and audiovisual markets and to the cinemas, to which we dedicate 17 million euros. Ladies and gentlemen, reading is perhaps the most personal, intimate cultural activity, of loneliness or encounter with oneself to learn, to entertain, to broaden our gaze. Well, the Directorate-General for the Book increases its budget by just over 30%. This increase is strongly linked to the Plan for the Promotion of Reading, of which I would like to highlight the provision of funds to public libraries, by purchasing through the bookstore system, for an amount of 1.8 million euros. It is a measure of reinforcement to the independent bookstores, to the neighborhood bookstores, to which we have already helped with the Decree of 5 May last, which are the heart of the entire chain of the book and which have suffered singularly. [[Applauds]] We also support the libraries, which we already have, and other forthcoming ones, such as the library of Vigo, the library of Barcelona, the library of Malaga or the library of Alicante. And together with this, we will close in the next few days a strategic agreement with three bands with Correos, with the booksellers and their representatives - with Cegal - to boost the book trade by reducing the shipping costs; an initiative with three clear objectives: to reduce costs to the booksellers in the shipments and also to the citizens in their purchases; to promote universal access to reading to the elderly who are at home, to the sick and also to the inhabitants of the Spanish interior; and finally to encourage reading, which is what makes us free and gives us a thought of our own, of that moral autonomy, of that majority of age with which the illustrated dreamed. The crisis has also had a virulent impact on the art galleries sector. That is why we will invest EUR 3 million in the increase in the state collections, in line with what we have already done with the decree of May and other subsequent aid. This is a highly demanded measure with the ultimate objective of supporting artists, but it will also serve to support our gallery sector. By the way, I encourage all institutions, administrations, agencies and companies to do the same; let us promote Spanish art and Spanish galleries. Similarly, the protection and safeguarding of cultural and intangible heritage receives an increase of EUR 340 000 in aid. The music bands of the Valencian Community or the belenists will be two of the next additions to this rich and diverse Intangible Heritage of our country. [[Applauds]] On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to reaffirm the responsibility that this ministry, the Ministry of Culture and Sport, has in maintaining the ecosystem of cultural institutions in the country. We are talking about twenty-five state centres, archives and museums located throughout Spain, which are a national and international reference in their fields of specialization and which are a fundamental element of territorial cohesion. Museums preserve our identity, testify to our history and disseminate it rigorously, making known to young and older people the evolution of culture to this day. But they are also key for their association with one of the main economic drivers of our country: tourism, what we call cultural tourism. And Spain is a world power. For this reason, and to put just a few examples, in these General State Budgets we propose extraordinary transfers for our great Prado Museum, which will receive 13 million euros extra; for the Reina Sofia National Museum, which increases its budget by 11 million or for the Thyssen Collection Foundation which experiences an increase of almost 2.5 million euros. The stage centers themselves will also receive 6.5 million and the matches for the country's major lyrical centers - the Teatro Real, the Liceu de Barcelona, the Maestranza de Sevilla, the Palau de les Arts de Valencia or the Opera de Bilbao - experience an overall increase of more than 4.5 million euros. Other cultural institutions, such as the Joan Miró Foundation, the Academy of Cinema, the Circle of Fine Arts, the IVAM of Valencia, the San Sebastián Film Festival, the Heritage City of Mérida or the Centro Nacional del Vidrio de Segovia Foundation, are being improved their aid, as well as those intended for works throughout the national territory, by all the autonomous communities. Ladies and gentlemen, the Government of Spain understands that the cultural and creative industries are a strategic sector of our economy and the incorporation of digital technology into its dynamics must act as an important accelerator of its transformative capacity. It is a question of promoting culture in its dimension of wealth-generating and employment-generating industry; employment that must also be of quality, with stability and legal certainty, that will flourish and be visible, with all our recognition, and here the development and culmination of the artist's status will be fundamental. [[Applauds]] For this reason, and to give just a few examples, we have created a new line of support for the development of video games and digital creation, for an amount of EUR 1 million. The relationship between culture and technology has its most paradigmatic expression in the video game sector. This effort to modernize and digitize must always be accompanied by respect for copyright and intellectual property rights. The search for a more efficient management of the documentation systems of works, of the licenses or of the division of the rights is the reason why our budgets include transfers of 10 million euros for projects of digitization of the collective management entities, in a clear demonstration of the support and commitment of this ministry with those entities, with all of them. Of course we create a line of aids for the digitisation of the entire chain of the book - publishers, distributors, booksellers - for the amount of 8.2 million euros. Digitization also of the archives of the National Library of Spain, increasing its budget by 3 million euros. Support programme for creation and training promoted by the ICAA in the amount of EUR 10.1 million. We also reserve 4.2 million euros for the analysis of data of the immense and most valuable documentary fund of our National Film Library, of the Spanish film library. Ladies and gentlemen, let us now also talk about sport. This Government regards sport as a right, it said so at the beginning of my intervention. Sport is health, physical and mental; it is the link between peoples and between hobbies; it is well-being; it favors tourism; it creates industry and generates employment. It is a mark of our country outwards, where we are recognized and valued as a fundamental sporting power, especially in team sports. Ladies and gentlemen, we are much better than we sometimes believe. This speech also translates into figures in this budget with the aim of protecting all its social fabric, economically reactivating the sector and driving the four major transformations that our sport needs for the future: digitization, energy transition, strengthening the link between health and sport - health, sport and clean play - and betting on women's sport, basic sport, university sport and inclusive sport. To this end, the CSD will have a budget of 251 million in 2021, to which will be added 9.4 million for the Spanish Agency for the Protection of Health in Sport, in a fight against doping, that is, against the dirty game, which we will reinforce with the new law in 2021. There is a bet decided by the sports federations as an essential instrument for the development of competitive sport in our country. We respect them in their autonomy and we need them and value them. For this reason, its budget increases by 23.37 % to EUR 66.3 million. To this allocation, the transfers derived from the proceeds from the sale of audiovisual rights must be added. In a circumstantially Olympic year, the Spanish Government strongly supports the activity of the Spanish Olympic Committee, increasing its allocation by 3.8 million euros, resulting in a total of 4.7 million euros, and that of the Spanish Paralympic Committee up to 1.9 million euros, with an increase of 1.5; we went from 400 000 euros to almost 2 million euros. Ladies and gentlemen, as I said to you, support for women's sport is also very important. I will give you a single figure, adding all that is devoted to women's sport, that is, to the promotion of equality in sport, the Ministry of Culture and Sport, through the CSD, will dedicate for next year 15.7 million euros, which represent an increase of 540 % over the previous year. We also want to support the work of the autonomous communities and, therefore, transfers to these administrations grow by 30%, reaching EUR 14.6 million. Similarly, for the improvement of their sports facilities, the municipalities and the deputies will be increased by 876 %, from just over EUR 500 000 to EUR 5 million. Ladies and gentlemen, in the recent words of the President of the Government, these General State Budgets represent the best vaccine against the virus of injustice, unemployment or precarious employment and lack of growth. Culture is necessary and also needs us. We bet on it, as these budgets do, without doubt, and also on sport because we bet on rights, the economy, employment, the prestige of Spain and the best values we have cultivated and built throughout our history: values of humanity, civilization and coexistence. This is culture too, ladies and gentlemen. Vote for these budgets by thinking of the Spanish. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u93	Thank you very much, Mr Vice-President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear today before the plenary of this House to present to you the section of the draft General Budgets of the State concerning the Ministry of Health. Let me first establish an important premise. So far the government and all the public administrations have faced the pandemic by COVID-19 with the overall budgets for 2018. This has not prevented us from making an unprecedented deployment of public funds, as the situation required us: the COVID funds, the minimum living income, the framework agreement for the acquisition of health supplies. But it is crucial to have new public accounts and the State's General Budgets are more than ever the most powerful lever to confront the pandemic, not only in health, but also in economic and social terms. At a time when redistributive policies will - they are already - be of vital importance, having general budgets commensurate with the circumstances means providing citizens, and especially the most vulnerable citizens, with a fundamental instrument in defending their rights and equal opportunities. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, the General Budgets of the State are the best guarantors of equal opportunities in access to public services that help us all to cope with this pandemic, and health is at the core of these public services. No one escapes that the pandemic is a before and after, also in the social configuration itself. Therefore, the need for budgets is not only to deal with the virus by ensuring human and material resources against the disease and to distribute vaccines, but also to support the public authorities so that no one is left unprotected. The pandemic today demands an unequivocal response from all elements of the welfare state: education, protection from unemployment, care for dependents and, obviously, health care. Ladies and gentlemen, public social protection is the ultimate objective of the draft General State Budgets that the Government has brought to this House, and the health section of them responds to that objective in three ways. Firstly, giving a unique and strong financial response to the worst health crisis in the last hundred years, with an appropriate budget. Secondly, giving scope for the so-called European funds - the React-EU and those of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism - to deploy their full effectiveness in protecting the health of citizens, updating the National Health System and driving a shift towards a more robust, flexible and resilient model that is capable of responding adequately to current and future challenges. In this respect, let me tell you that even without these European funds the budget for the Ministry of Health would grow by 40%. The commitment to strengthening, therefore, is clear. Thirdly, by strengthening the role of the Ministry of Health without prejudice or, rather, for the benefit of a decentralized system of competences. These three routes for public health protection are covered by EUR 3421 million of Section 26 of the budget, which corresponds to the Ministry of Health. They amount to EUR 3094 million more, an increase of 946.8 %, and turn the Ministry of Health into one of the fastest growing in percentage terms. Some groups in this House have expressed their scepticism with these accounts, while refusing to acknowledge the need for new public accounts. There have been many others who, aware of the serious moment we are in, have made contributions and amendments, and I would like to thank you from here for your constructive work. Ladies and gentlemen, for a serious moment there are strong, robust, unequivocal budgets that strengthen the cohesion, equity, quality and accessibility of our health system. There are four issues that very well outline the budgetary commitment in the area of health: firstly, the very important support for the National Health System and the Strategic Framework for Primary and Community Care; secondly, the clear impetus for digital health and technological renewal; thirdly, the recovery and extension of rights; and fourthly, the public response to the pandemic. We'll derail these matters one by one. With regard to the National Health System, autonomous health services will receive EUR 1088 million, which is a key injection for the implementation of the Strategic Framework for Primary and Community Care agreed at the time by the Interterritorial Council of the National Health System. These more than EUR 1 billion are the support of the Spanish Government, in a coordination scenario, for basic needs that must be addressed by the autonomous communities. Secondly, digital health - which has already had a decisive boost in the new organizational chart of the ministry with the creation of the General Secretariat for Digital Health, Information and Innovation of the National Health System - is now given a second decisive boost in the same direction. The Government wants the potentialities of digitisation to be fully deployed in the field of health, in prevention, surveillance, diagnosis, treatment and health management. Our country already occupies a leading European place in electronic health, but it is not enough and not only for the tasks that we still have pending and that the pandemic has revealed, but also because the constant technological evolution opens up new possibilities for application in the health field. With two large figures, ladies and gentlemen, we can all see this extraordinary progress in modernization taking place. First, the National Health System's Health Technology Renewal Plan will receive an injection of €400 million. Secondly, we will make an ambitious bet on digital transformation, health information systems and epidemiological surveillance with EUR 200 million. The third point of attention is in the area of citizenship rights. These budgets are committed to strengthening the National Health System and, in particular, primary care. They put an end to years of cuts, they make very clear progress in terms of technological renewal and digital transformation, but also in a very clear way they return to the path of citizens' rights not only in terms of recovering lost rights, but also - and I say this with great illusion and with great ambition - in terms of extending rights. There was a time when the financial response to the crises only covered austerity policies. The whole of the European Union has learned that the appropriate response to a crisis situation is in the opposite direction, that of expanding the area of citizenship rights and the social protection of a robust welfare State. These budgets, also in health, are the children of this conclusion that Europe is going through in this crisis. Thus, the draft budgets submitted for consideration by this House include measures to reverse these years of rights cuts. First, the National Health System's portfolio of services will be progressively expanded in the area of oral health by EUR 48 million. Secondly, the most vulnerable groups will see the one known as a pharmaceutical copayo removed, which was introduced in 2012. Thus, three collectives, minors with a degree of disability equal to or greater than 33%, who receive a social security benefit per child or minor under permanent family care or foster care for adoption purposes and pensioners with incomes below certain levels - amounting to more than 6 million people - will benefit from universal and fair access to medicines thanks to the fact that this draft budget provides for the corresponding amendment of Law 29/2006 on guarantees and rational use of medicines and health products. Ladies and gentlemen, the fourth axis is the public response to the pandemic. Obviously, the commitment to primary and community care, equipment modernisation or digital transformation also make up the response to this pandemic, but I would like to highlight a number of specific measures. Firstly, the acquisition of COVID vaccines will be provided with more than EUR 1 billion, so that all citizens will have access to a safe vaccine through the National Health System. The budget of the State Agency for Medicines and Sanitary Products will be increased by 20 million euros to facilitate the purchase for the strategic reserve. And the State Centre for Public Health, whose establishment we have already committed to at parliamentary level, will have its launch backed by an investment of €5 million, a fundamental effort in public health that we have found to be crucial to increasing the resilience of the system to threats to collective health. Finally, I would like to mention to you another series of budget increases that we can also be proud of, in my view. External current transfers, that is, those that finance actions of autonomous communities, local corporations and non-profit entities, will be increased by more than 2700 %, to almost 793 million euros. We reincorporate the Health Cohesion Fund into the General Budgets of the State, suspended since 2013, which is fundamental to the cohesion of the system. The strengthening of the Cohesion Fund focuses on compensating for the costs of health care provided in the centres, services or reference units, the CSFs, with a budget of 66.86 million, of which 30 million correspond to the Cohesion Fund and 36.86 million to finalist expenditure on cohesion and equity projects of the European Union. The National Institute of Health Management will have an initial budget of 323 million euros, in which the real investments amount to more than 40 million euros, an increase of 294%. Transfers to the National Transplant Organisation will be increased by almost €7 million, an increase of 13.4%. With regard to the National Drug Plan, three out of four Euros of the European funding received will be allocated to the plans and programmes of the Autonomous Communities and Cities and the programmes for the prevention of local corporations. The new additions, for their part, will receive financial support of €7.23 million. Ladies and gentlemen, in conclusion, we are continuing a path of reversal of cuts, recovery of rights and expansion of the service portfolio; millions of fellow citizens, especially the most vulnerable, will notice this when they go to the pharmacy and see that they do not have to pay for their medicines. We make a very important effort in digitization, modernization and technological renewal; we lay a very important foundation for a real digital revolution in the National Health System. We strengthen primary and community care for the benefit of citizens and the entire National Health System; it is not only a state response to strengthen the level of care, which acts as a backbone of the system and which the pandemic has shown us to be essential, but it is a commitment determined by a system that is closer to people and their social realities in order to better care for their health. The response to the pandemic is, in financial terms, broad and strong, including absolute public coverage of the vaccination process. In this regard, ladies and gentlemen, today I would like to announce that in order to report on all the actions relating to the vaccination by COVID-19 I have requested my appearance in a monographic way in the Committee on Health and Consumer Affairs in this House, so they will have completed information on this process in the coming weeks. Ladies and gentlemen, on previous occasions I have invited you to join in the fight against the pandemic by COVID-19, today I invite you to join in an ambitious path, that of a strengthened and modernized national health system, a national health system strengthened in the face of adversity, which remains the powerful democratic tool facilitating equity and cohesion in the country, which is a renewed pillar of the welfare state, a welfare state that many had called into question but which the pandemic has shown is essential to protect the most basic area of the rights of all citizens. Those of us who are on this path today will always remember with pride that our responsibility has been exercised in a tangible and lasting benefit to the citizenry. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u108	We continue with the budget debate, in this case section 27, Ministry of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation. For the section presentation, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, the third Vice-President of the Government and Minister for Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u109	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before this House today, as my fellow government officials have already done in recent days, to defend section 27 of the State General Budgets Bill for 2021. I am not going to go too far, since the detail of this section has already been presented by the Secretaries of State of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation, but I am going to spend a little time explaining the macroeconomic picture underlying and accompanying the draft General Budgets of the State, and then briefly reviewing the most important elements of the Ministry's budget. With regard to the macroeconomic picture, as I have already had occasion to say in this same forum on other occasions, we are at a time marked by the intense uncertainty, both in Spain and in Europe and the rest of the world, and therefore the macroeconomic picture accompanying the draft budget contains prudent forecasts, which contemplate the possible downward risks associated with a more negative evolution of the pandemic, a possible delay in the availability of the vaccine, an increase in geopolitical and commercial tensions, the possibility of a structural impact on the productive fabric or the employment that slows the recovery, and the possible difficulties in implementing European funds in an agile and complete manner. However, since the publication of our forecasts in early October, new information has become known. There have been vaccine-related announcements indicating that the vaccine could be available for supply to the population since the beginning of January; there have also been encouraging developments in relation to geopolitical and commercial tensions; the third quarter of gross domestic product and the labour force survey, which was a positive surprise for all analysts, public and private, national and international. Indeed, with regard to the fourth quarter's advanced indicators, in which the second wave arrived in Spain before other European countries and intensified from mid-October, leading to the imposition of additional containment measures in some autonomous communities, what we see is that the incidence of the pandemic in recent weeks seems to have returned - let alone with all caution - to a declining path that is being accompanied by a progressive lifting of some of these local restrictions. However, the signals offered by the high-frequency indicators that we follow daily on this fourth quarter can be considered mixed, with some indicators more affected by the partial restrictions that have been imposed, such as expenditure indicators, in the face of a positive evolution of labour indicators, such as the social security affiliation and registered unemployment data that we have seen this morning. In fact, the social security affiliation data for the month of November, which we have seen this morning, show an increase in the employment of more than 100 000 people in deseasonalized, calendar-corrected terms, which double the rate of job creation of 50 000 jobs created in October, also in deseasonalized terms; a positive tone that remains at the start of this month of December. In short, of the million jobs lost between March and April, some 650 000, about two thirds have already been recovered, and we have recovered the 19 million members. Self-employed workers also outperform pre-pandemic membership and this good evolution of affiliations is compounded by the behaviour of the temporary employment regulation files, the ERTEs, which still have around 750 000 workers, a figure which remains fairly stable despite the flow of inputs and outputs resulting from the response of the various autonomous communities to the outbreak of the pandemic. Since mid-November, we have seen net outflows from these ERTs of some 10,000 workers on average per day. It is early to have a close view of the quarter, because there is still almost a month ahead and uncertainty remains very high, but, according to the information available, the economic impact of this second wave is well below that of March and April, which is good news for all. Thus, our central scenario foresees that a positive evolution will be maintained in this fourth quarter and that the recovery initiated in the third quarter will continue, taking into account, as I say, all the cautions and all the necessary preventions in this situation of uncertainty that explains the disparity of forecasts of the various agencies. All this makes the most negative scenarios of forecasting seem to be moving away - always with all prudence - and, however, our forecast points out that, following an intense fall in activity in 2020, next year 2021 we will experience a very intense recovery, around 7%, so that the land lost in this exercise would begin to recover. This inertial growth planned for 2021 could be increased by two and three percentage points thanks to the full implementation of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan through public investments mobilized by European funds which, as you know, are already reflected in the draft budgets. So, beyond the short-term impact, the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan will allow us to increase the potential growth of our economy by more than 2%, allow us to create around 800 000 jobs and mobilise a private investment of around EUR 500 billion. This forecast of inertial growth will be underpinned by an intense reactivation of national demand, driven by the expansion of all components; private consumption, thanks to the protection of household disposable income and the revitalization of employment; private investment, also particularly intense in the field of equipment goods; we also foresee the recovery of the external sector and an improvement of the labour market. This macroeconomic development will allow us to reduce the government deficit to 7.7% by 2021 and also to start the path of reducing the government debt ratio on gross domestic product. In this respect, I would like to point out that the good management of the treasury, together with the good development of the financial markets, has allowed us to reduce by EUR 20 billion the extraordinary emissions during 2020 from the pandemic we had calculated in the spring; that is, that, compared to the 130 billion we expected at the time, it will finally be 110 billion of public debt that we will issue in order to be able to finance the extraordinary response that Spanish society as a whole is giving to protect the productive fabric, jobs and incomes of families. For next year, the financing needs of the Kingdom of Spain's Treasury will also be around EUR 110 billion, with a financial burden of around EUR 30 billion. This is an extremely prudent estimate. One of the main innovations we anticipate for 2021 in this field is the issue of between EUR 5000 and EUR 10 billion in green bonds, learning from the first success experience carried out by the Official Credit Institute in 2019 and 2020. With regard to the emissions strategy, the same priorities will be maintained in recent years, we will continue to reduce our financing costs in order to reduce the average cost of outstanding debt, which is at 1.85 % historical minimums, and to continue, in parallel, to extend the average life of the portfolio, which has also reached a historical maximum of 7.82 years. With this last I introduce the reference to the budget for 2021 of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation. This section 27 together amounts to EUR 5122 million, almost three times the 2018 extended budget, which is in force. This amount includes €3650 million from the Next Generation EU recovery mechanisms. The main objectives pursued by the budget allocations in section 27 are essentially: to encourage the digitisation of the productive fabric of our society, by deploying the Digital Spain Agenda 2025 presented on 23 July, and to maintain Spain's participation in multilateral financial institutions. Within the competence of the Ministry's undersecretariat and internal management, it is worth mentioning the digital job project, which will allow us to replace computer equipment and reach 65% of the workforce by 2021 and 100% of the workforce by 2022. I turn to the details of the Secretariat of State for Economy and Enterprise Support, which will have 849 million euros, directed in a majority way, as I pointed out a moment ago, to the contributions of Spain to the multilateral financial institutions, in order to maintain and strengthen our capacity to influence global economic governance. It also includes an item related to the payment of contributions to alleviate Greece's debt burden, in compliance with our commitments to our partners in the euro area. The bulk of the ministry's budget is part of our country's digitalization and connectivity policies, with a six-fold increase in current allocations in this area. As all your ladies and gentlemen know, our country's digitalisation roadmap is set out in that Digital Spain Agenda 2025, to which we will allocate €3750 million in 2021. Firstly, the Secretariat of State for Digitisation and Artificial Intelligence will have 3069 million euros, an unprecedented figure that will multiply by almost ten its current allocation; it will be financed mostly by European funds, and we will allocate these resources to the different axes of action that have been detailed by the Secretary of State at her appearance. Let me simply point out the more than EUR 1 billion for the promotion of digital skills, through key programmes such as those supporting basic digital skills for digital citizenship, especially for women and girls, a key element for closing the gender gap; EUR 331 million for training in our SMEs; EUR 150 million for the re-training of unemployed workers, with specific measures for each of the vulnerable groups; the Education in Digital programme, to equip the educational system with devices, digital resources; the adaptation of teachers' digital skills, and actions to apply artificial intelligence to personalized education; and the Uni-Digital programme, which will allocate EUR 26 million to promote progress and digitalisation in higher education, in university education. And it is that, ladies and gentlemen, the pandemic has made it clear to us that the hybrid model of digital and face-to-face education has arrived to stay, and we must ensure that this change does not lead to discrimination against certain groups or to a reduction in the possibilities of developing our educational system, which is undoubtedly one of the greatest strengths of our welfare state. [[The lady]] Another area of action of this State Secretariat is aimed at boosting entrepreneurship and digitisation of the business fabric, with 219 million from the SME Digitalisation Drive Plan; EUR 154 million in entrepreneurship, e.g. for the creation of the new national entrepreneurship office, to which the EUR 315 million must be added to promote digitalisation tractor projects in key sectors such as agri-food, digital health, smart tourism, sustainable mobility and e-commerce. This Secretariat of State will also invest EUR 370 million through the General Secretariat of Digital Administration in the digital transformation of public administrations; we plan to provide EUR 213 million for the drive of cybersecurity, and finally we will allocate EUR 330 million to launch the National Artificial Intelligence Strategy, which has been presented by the President of the Government this morning - we have brought copies to distribute to your ladies and gentlemen, I hope they will share them soon -. This is undoubtedly one of the most important, strategic and vertebral projects for the digitization of our country and to direct this whole process of transformation, putting people at the centre, guaranteeing the protection of our individual and collective rights and our constitutional values. In this regard, until 20 December, we have opened the public hearing on a draft digital rights charter so that our country will be at the forefront of the debate that is taking place at European and global level in this important area. Finally, the State Secretariat for Telecommunications and Digital Infrastructure will also see its budget multiplied by three. The actions of this Secretariat of State can be summarized in three key lines of action. Firstly, the plan for the connectivity of individuals, enterprises and territories, which will have EUR 583 million in the financial year 2021, with the aim of ensuring that 100 per cent of the Spanish population has digital connectivity of 100 megabytes per second in 2025, thus contributing to progress in social and territorial cohesion. None of your ladies and gentlemen misses the importance of digital connectivity for the territorial vertebration of the entire Spanish population and I know that it is a matter very close to many of your ladies and gentlemen, who are aware of the importance of this collective investment in the territory. Secondly, the impetus for the deployment of the 5G, which, with EUR 300 million of the budget in 2021, will focus on promoting the deployment of this technology where private operators take longer to arrive; developing actions in the field of road and rail corridors, with particular attention to the secondary roads, which are often the most neglected by the European Union, and promoting the use of the 5G of the sectoral digitisation tractor projects that I have mentioned. Yesterday the Council of Ministers approved precisely the two plans - connectivity and deployment of the 5G - that will be key to arbitrating and articulating public and private investment in these two areas. In fact, with the National Artificial Intelligence Strategy presented this morning and the connectivity and 5G plans presented yesterday, we are laying the foundations for three of the main axes of the Digital Spain Strategy 2025 and, thereby, also covering an important part of the digital chapter of our country's Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. The third and final line of action of the Secretariat of State for Telecommunications is to promote the value of our country as a European platform for business, investment and work in the audiovisual and video game fields, with a budget allocation of 109 million euros for 2021. Alongside these investment programmes, these State secretariats will, of course, promote all the structural reforms that will enable us to address this process of transformation and resilience in our country. In the case of telecommunications, mention should be made of the telecommunications bills and the audiovisual bill, which are precisely at the stage of public hearing and which I hope we can bring here in the near future. President, I'm done. Ladies and gentlemen, two and a half years ago this House witnessed the last debate of this nature. Nobody escapes that the situation we are moving into today has absolutely nothing to do with the May 2018 situation. Only this fact and the year that we have been doing should be sufficient for us all to be aware of our responsibility and to guarantee the adoption of the General Budgets of the State which are essential. They are essential budgets for Spain, because they involve the mobilization of 39 billion euros of public investments, mostly financed by European funds, which offer us a historic opportunity to face transformations that we all recognize, which the vast majority of the Spanish population considers urgent and inescapable and which we now have the opportunity to launch in record time, supporting us for this in public-private collaboration. They are also essential budgets for our country because they help to give an image of credibility and trust to the rest of the world, that is, in themselves they are a value for this country and help us to continue to drive the economy and, with it, to create jobs. These budgets are ambitious, they are expansive, they are social and they are transformative of reality. They are committed to the priority areas in which our country must stand as a global vanguard in order to win the future for our young people. These are budgets whose priorities are shared by the vast majority of the Spanish population and should therefore be supported by all parties in this House. We have a shared responsibility. That is why I urge all of you to take it by voting in favour of the General Budgets of the State for 2021. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

This situation implies that the same profits, when they reach the matrix, if there are three companies interposed in the holding, will pay an amount, and if there are five companies interposed, they will pay a higher amount, because it is accumulating. We've passed it on to the Ministry of Finance, which I understand is studying it. It would be a modification for everyone's benefit. It would have an impact on collection, which would be very little, but otherwise the impact on business organization would be much worse. There is also an amendment which was voted on yesterday and which did not come forward by five votes - which gives one a little pain - relating simply to the adjustment of the diesel vehicle registration tax. ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-02-CD201202.u125	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear to set out the general lines of my department's budgets for 2021. During this vicious COVID-19 pandemic, we have all been aware of the key importance of science and innovation. Scientific knowledge and researchers are clear protagonists of this time. Progress made and its practical implementation are present in the day-to-day discussions. Another idea that has emerged strongly is the need to have innovative companies capable of translating all that knowledge into society and, in this case, to provide us with essential supplies without external dependencies. The need for a State policy for science and innovation, a policy worthy of maximum consensus and far from partyism, had never been so evident, because it would be essential to accelerate recovery and, beyond that, to ensure long-term prosperity. In this House we have already achieved in the past consensus on strengthening our science and innovation, as was the unanimous adoption of a Royal Decree-Law on urgent measures to support the research system in March 2019. The budgets of the Ministry of Science and Innovation that I am going to present to you are a unique opportunity to recover ground and put our system up to the level of the most advanced countries in our environment, with the prospect of at least converging in the medium term with the average investment of the European Union. In the following, I will mention figures on non-financial items. The ministry also has financial items that are used for various purposes, but in the past confusion has been created by mixing or adding both together and it seems to me clearer today to expose only the real resources available, the so-called chapters 1 to 7. The ministry's budgets have been designed with two objectives. The first objective, which could be implemented and absorbed by the system. The execution of the ministry and its agencies is above 90% and these budgets are designed to continue to be so. The games are also tailored to the capabilities of the science and innovation system to take advantage of them efficiently. And the second objective is for them to have a short-term or at least medium-term impact, in line with the European Union's demands for recovery funds. Two aspects have prevailed: on the one hand, the recovery of the capacities of the system so affected by the previous crisis, and on the other, we have placed emphasis on the impact of programmes on the productive system through the transfer of knowledge. Budgets for this department represent the largest direct investment in R & D+i in absolute terms in our history, 59.4% more than the budgets for this year, and are over €32 billion, above the previous record of €25 billion in 2009. They are the continuation, and in part the implementation, of the shock plan that I presented in July with more than EUR 1 billion of extraordinary commitments for 2020-2021; they represent an increase greater than the total increase in the general state budgets, as we have said, 59.4% compared to 41%, which is what increases the global accounts, which demonstrates this government's commitment to the research and innovative fabric. Ladies and gentlemen, these budgets are in line with the conclusions of the Commission for Social and Economic Reconstruction which you adopted last July and which proposed - I quote literally - to increase public investment in civil R & D above the increase in the non-financial expenditure ceiling of the State and to move towards the Community's objectives of total public and private investment. In allocating budget increases, we have been guided by the following priorities. The first priority is to increase calls for funding agencies for science and innovation, in particular for public-private collaboration and knowledge transfer, with economic and social benefits, consolidating the instruments put in place and launching new ones. The second priority is to strengthen the recruitment of research staff with a view to reforming the Science Act, which is currently in public consultation. It envisages the creation of a stable figure of entry into the system, the so-called tenoretrack, which most countries in our environment have. It is a reform aimed at reducing the precariousness and temporality of our young scientists and which affects women in particular. Third priority is the modernisation of the country's leading scientific infrastructures, such as the renovation and improvement of animal testing laboratories in vaccines and treatments and major European national infrastructures. And fourth priority: funding strategic projects with tractor capacity and economic impact, such as personalized medicine or energy transformation in the face of climate change. The budgets also include some regulatory and administrative changes. First, they amend Law 40/2015 on the public sector so that state agencies such as the CSIC and the State Research Agency and entities such as the CDTI can continue with their management particularities that ensure their operation. Secondly, they give a strong impetus to improving the efficiency of project calls and the science, technology and innovation information system, through investments in digitisation of these systems. And, thirdly, they also support scientific and technological parks, which will be re-engineered at twenty-five years old, provided that they demonstrate their viability. I shall go on to detail some of the main developments in this budget. With regard to the increase in calls from the funding agencies for science and innovation in our country, the Centre for Technological and Industrial Development, the CDTI, increases its budget by 445 million - as we have said, not financial - by 42% more than in 2020. It highlights the new fund-sharing initiative for technology-based companies from the research centre, which, together with the funds earmarked for co-investment through the Investment Facility, will receive EUR 217 million by 2021. A new €30 million programme is also envisaged to support innovative SMEs that have received the European Seal of Excellence, accompanying them in their market consolidation phase. In addition, two new instruments launched in the past two years will be continued: the Science and Innovation Missions programme and the Cervera Network between technology centres and small and medium-sized enterprises. We have allocated a total of EUR 140 million to these programmes. For our part, we have increased the resources of the State Research Agency by 29% to an amount of EUR 825 million. In fact, if we meet their commitments to pay for the calls planned for 2021, these - the payment commitments - will double with those for 2020. The increase in resources will be aimed, in addition to consolidating the increase in the general call, at research projects aimed at the transfer of knowledge, to promote and co-finance European cooperation and to human resources programmes which I will then detail. I would now like to refer to the Carlos III Institute of Health, the country's leading health research institution. My department's bet is that this institution will increase its calls by 55 million euros, both for strategic health action and in a call for the renewal of scientific equipment. As I was telling you, another of our priorities is to regain opportunities to attract and retain talent. For this reason, we will allocate more than €60 million more for various human resources programmes, including calls for the incorporation of doctoral students, postdoctoral programmes and those promoting the recruitment of doctoral students in companies and technology centres. In addition, R & D+i will be the priority sector for public employment provision in 2021, with a replacement rate of 110 %. As regards the modernisation of scientific infrastructures, I will inform you of some of the most outstanding. Firstly, we will resume funding on the map of unique scientific and technical facilities with EUR 49.6 million. These are 29 major infrastructures in our country, which have been running since 2011 without all the funding provided. Secondly, we will provide the greatest funding in history to the National Supercomputing Centre, the BSC, in the implementation phase of one of the major supercomputers of the European High Performance Computing Programme. State contributions to the Bilbao Spalling Source, the National Research Centre on Human Evolution in Burgos, and also to the Alba Syncrotron will also be significantly increased. Thirdly, we will allocate EUR 31.6 million to ensure greater Spanish representation and participation in large European research infrastructures, for example, participation in new projects at the CERN, in the Geneva Syncrotron. Finally, we wanted to bet on financing strategic projects with tractor capacity and economic impact; this adds up to more than 350 million euros in emerging areas and in collaboration with the autonomous communities, which will continue in 2022 and 2023. I'd like to value some of them. Firstly, a new joint programming instrument in R & D+i with the Autonomous Communities, to which the Government will allocate EUR 100 million by 2021. Secondly, more than EUR 140 million for concrete actions in health research: first, in the Personalized Medicine Strategy; second, the strengthening of the two centres of the Carlos III Institute dedicated to the fight against COVID-19, the National Microbiology Centre and the National Epidemiology Centre; and third, the Platform for comprehensive approach to pandemics proposed by the CSIC. These are the concrete actions in health research. And, thirdly, research and transfer projects in energy areas, with more than €90 million for the modernisation of infrastructure in the Ciemat: a new energy storage centre in Extremadura in collaboration with the Board; research programmes in new storage technologies with Redox Flow; and study of carbon dioxide capture techniques, among others. Ladies and gentlemen, so far the summary of the budgets of the Ministry of Science and Innovation. They are robust and realistic budgets; they represent a very different response to previous crises where funding was cut to these areas.
